ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28195	Thank you, Mr. President of the Parliament. Dear ladies and gentlemen of Parliament. Here are three laws, energy laws that are very important and above all, which are changes to the energy law and new laws on electricity triangulation and the law on regulation of energy activities. Refort energy sector in the Republic of Croatia began in July 2001 when the Croatian Parliament adopted the concept of reform adopted a package of energy laws. This enables, among other things, the creation of an institutional energy sector framework with particular emphasis on energy market liberalisation. The five Directives of the European Union that came into force after 2001 are newly regulating issues in the energy sector. The additional reason for amending these laws is also the difficulties in implementing the energy sector reform under existing laws. It is necessary to redesign an existing system and market operator by setting up a transmission system operator to perform integrated power system management and power transmission functions. It is necessary to separate the market operator from HEP. Such a solution brings unique responsibility for the security of the electricity system and is in line with solutions in most European countries. Further opening of the market requires a more operational energy regulator. So short a few sentences about each of these laws. Amendments to the Energy Act. The energy law is a roof law for all laws in the energy sector and provides a comprehensive legal framework for the sector. This law sets out, first and foremost, the structure of regulated and free energy prices. Methodology for the determination of tariff items with a height of headings. And jurisdiction over its adoption which is reasonably assigned to the Government, the Ministry of Regulatory and Energy Entities to which a particular tariff system is concerned. The law also accurately formulate general conditions for energy supply and consumer protection measures. To speed up solving the problem of illegal construction, these amendments to the law prohibit the collection of buildings with a construction permit on energy networks. The electricity market law lays down regulated and marketable electricity activities. How to obtain the status of a privileged producer, and the right to encourage. As well as the procedures for granting approval for the construction of new power plants, and the publication and execution of a public tender if access is to be opened for that construction. The law lays down the responsibilities and responsibilities of transmission operator and distribution system operator. AND their independence in a vertically integrated company. The independence of electricity loads from electricity transmission and distribution activities and the obligation of all suppliers to assume electricity from renewable energy sources and generation as well as the obligation of public information providers is regulated. Special emphasis is on the protection of customers from the category of households and small customers. Additional roles of market operators in the performance of operations, e.g. the calculation of balancing energy, and the collection and distribution of funds for the promotion of renewable energy sources and cogeneration have been established. The annual spending limit has been reduced to obtaining preferential customer status, from 40 to 20 gigawatt hours, and dynamics have been established until the market is finally opened. The public service obligation of supply and the rights and obligations of privileged and tariff buyers has been established in a transparent manner. The transitional and final provisions of the law lay down the obligations of HEP for the provision of public services and for the establishment of a new structure, in particular the separation of supply and distribution. Issuing of market operators from HEP and public service obligations of customers who are left without suppliers or who do not wish to use the status of a privileged buyer. In order to establish and implement the regulation of energy activities, the system of regulated action of energy subjects, and the establishment and arrangement of the energy market by the new energy regulation law, it is proposed to establish an independent, independent, non-profit, public institutions called the Croatian Energy Regulatory Agency. The founder of the agency is proposed that the Republic of Croatia, the agency for its work, corresponds to the Croatian Assembly, this law regulates the internal structure of the agency, accurately determines and determines its business and activities as an activity of special state interest. For example, these are the tasks of disposing of permits to carry out energy activities. By providing methodologies and supervision of the application of tariff systems. Control of the work of energy subjects. All of the so far, this is important to say, the acts of the Council for the Regulatory of Energy Activities and all the solutions of that Council and the permits are valid for the time of the one issued, so it simply ensures the continuity of the current council and the new agency. The reasons why this law is important and important to adopt already and this year are not only a alignment with the European Union directives but also our international obligations and the need to be members of European associations so that we can use the funds, that we are moving towards the liberalisation of the energy sector as quickly as possible. To regulate the position of renewable energy sources and cogenerations that have not been legally regulated so far. In view of the need to establish a level of operationality in the work of regulators and normal compliance with the way energy law, electricity market law and energy regulation law. Therefore, I ask you to adopt these amendments to the law on energy and new laws on the electricity market and the law on energy regulation. Thank you.

The Committee on the Economy, Development and Reconstruction of the Croatian Parliament at the 21st session held on 29 November 2004 discussed the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law on energy with the Final Proposal of the Law, the proposal for a regulation of energy activities with the Final Proposal for a Law and the Proposal for a Law on the Market of Electricity with the Final Proposal for Law, which was submitted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia by the Act of 18 November 2004, with a proposal to comply with Article 159. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament of Laws shall be adopted by an urgent procedure. The Committee shall be within the meaning of Article 67. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament discussed the above-mentioned Bills as a parent working body. On a proposal from the Committee president, the Committee discussed the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Energy Act with the Final Proposal for Law, the emergency procedure, the 1st and 2nd reading, the proposal for a law on the electricity market with the final draft law, the emergency procedure, the 1st and 2nd reading and the proposal for a law on energy regulation with the final draft law, the emergency procedure 1st and 2nd reading. In his opening statement, the applicant's representative pointed out that the regulatory energy activity process started in 2001 and that, after a short delay in these legislative proposals, the process continued. When drawing up these legislative proposals, binding guidelines of the European Union have been taken into account, in particular those 5 relating to these legislative proposals. The applicant specifically pointed out that according to the European Union directives, the energy system should be guided in a way that enables basic decisions to be made in one place. These legislative proposals prescribe guidelines for other laws to be passed and are related to energy activity. A request for a structured energy price has also been introduced, so that all consumers know what enters that price. Particularly protected are natural persons and small businesses, as energy users. It is prescribed that all users have the right to choose to buy electricity. The proposing representative also stressed the jurisdiction of the Croatian regulatory agency whose decisions are final, which means that only judicial protection can be sought against them. These legislative proposals have established a new way of calculating the transmission of electricity abroad. The adoption of these laws is a presumption of the adoption of sublegal acts in the field of energy which should result in greater liberalisation of the energy market. In the opening of the debate, the Committee President met the members of the Committee with the material of the Croatian Chamber of Commerce whose representative pointed out that HEP supports the adoption of the proposed laws, while INA and JANAF have objections which are united in that material. The basic observation by INA and JANAF is that, in view of the importance of the matter regulated by these legislative proposals, they should go into the regular procedure of adoption without the application of an emergency procedure. The representative of the Croatian Chamber of Commerce has proposed that the fundamental law relating to the harmonisation of Croatian legislation with the legislation of the European Union should also be discussed in regular procedure. The president of the trade union in Croatia's electricity industry stressed that these legal proposals begin the abolition of the HEP monopoly and continue the process of restructuring of HEP. Following the discussion, the Committee unanimously reached the next conclusion. It is supported by the adoption of the Law amending and supplementing the Energy Act, the adoption of the Energy Regulation Act and the adoption of the Electricity Market Act. Thank you.
ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28200	Thank you very much, Mr. President. I'm opening a club debate. Croatian Democratic Community Representatives' Club, respected representative Kresimir Cosic. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28201	Your Honor, Mr. President, Mr. Secretary, ladies and gentlemen. The 2001 Energy Act, as known, is a roof law for all other laws in the field of energy. It was prepared nearly 5 years ago, and it is understandable today that it is not in line with the new European Union directives adopted in 2003 and there are legitimate reasons for updating it. Because of this, it is not enough framework for all other laws in the energy sector and the efforts of the Government of Croatia to modernise and update it have been fully justified and therefore here today we are discussing this package of laws. I'll just point out some shortcomings in this law. The current Energy Act provides that energy activities are separate from the transmission of electricity and the management of the electricity system. That's not good for many reasons. Not only because of the fact that this does not correspond to European knowledge today, but simply the necessary level of synergy and uniform management of the electricity system management system and the transmission system enables far greater efficiency of the system as a whole. Furthermore, Directive 203 54 EC does not know a separate system operator from an energy transmission operator, but only a single transmission system operator, tsl TSO transmission system operator to be responsible for the following tasks: ensuring the long-term capacity of the demand system, contributing to security of supply, managing energy flows in the network, informing the operator of adjacent networks, non-discrimination of network users, informing network users etc. In addition, there are additional reasons why it was necessary to update the existing energy law. A very significant problem of rationalising energy consumption, i.e. The increase in energy efficiency is not sufficiently defined and regulated by the current Energy Act and no compliance with the European Union directives has been achieved. The relevant law further does not adequately and sufficiently clearly describe the tariff systems and the procedures for their adoption for certain energy activities. Furthermore, the applicable gas economy law does not cover the energy activity of natural gas supply. In addition, natural gas supply and natural gas distribution activities as public services do not have a regulated price tariff methodology. Furthermore, as regards oil and petroleum products, the applicable law does not include the energy activities of biofuel production, oil pipeline transport, oil and oil transport, and oil derivatives and biofuels by road vehicles. This is due to the fact that the energy operators present cannot obtain authorisations to carry out the activities for which they are otherwise qualified. Furthermore, the adoption of this law and all the laws that we are discussing today, which are related to this law, will result in the opening of the energy market according to the European Union directives and will contribute to the rapid liberalisation of the energy sector and the rationalisation of energy entities in the Republic of Croatia. I will stress only some important elements relating to harmonisation with European Union directives and documents, the adoption of common rules on the electricity market, the adoption of common rules on the internal market for natural gas, the adoption and promotion of the use of electricity from renewable energy sources, the promotion of cogeneration based on the use of useful heat and the promotion of the use of biofuels and other renewable fuels in transport. So from all that's been said, it's quite clear that for the past five years they've made important changes in the existing Energy Act. So the main objective of this bill is to better identify and regulate all energy activities in the Republic of Croatia, ensure a unique view of all energy activities, a single management and management system with all the important parts of this complex energy system in the Republic of Croatia. Establish a more comprehensive legal framework for other energy sector laws in order to make production, transfer, distribution and supply of energy to all customers in the Republic of Croatia more reliable and efficient. In addition, all documents place particular emphasis on environmental protection measures, renewable energy, cogeneration and energy efficiency. Now allow me a few words about the proposal for a law on energy regulation. Recently in this House, we discussed the Council's annual report on energy regulation. We took note of it, with certain observations which were primarily related to the fact that the Energy Regulatory Council has, in accordance with the existing law, significant means to charge for the provision and authorisation of activities in the energy sector. However, this Energy Regulatory Council had only three employees with such significant revenues and I personally suggested that we be provided with a report on the financial operations of the Energy Regulatory Council, but here we are today discussing a proposal for a law on the regulation of energy activities which, in some ways, dodging or abolishing the Energy Regulatory Council, which really makes sense and justification, I would like to say a few words. The Republic of Croatia is a signator of the European Energy Charter, which has been established to accelerate the liberalisation of the energy sector and allow third parties to access existing transmission and transport systems of energy in the Republic of Croatia. The aim of opening the market is to give all energy suppliers an equal opportunity to supply customers with objectively visible and impartial conditions, and the establishment of regulation should allow for control of the achievement of the objectives set. The proposed law, as I said about regulation of energy activities, establishes and this is highlighted in the opening section, the Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities as a public and independent institution that is structured as follows. She has her own board of directors, she needs to have her own board president, she has an agency, i.e. staff of the Agency and she should have a director as an appropriate professional. The Administrative Board of the Energy Regulatory Agency shall be appointed by the Croatian Parliament. I hope that we will have this very soon in the Croatian Parliament in the procedure, and the director is elected by the administrative council as an expert in the public competition. The Statute of the Agency shall be adopted by the Administrative Board of the Agency, with the prior consent of the Government of the Republic of Croatia. The activities of the Agency shall be of particular interest to the Republic of Croatia and shall be carried out by the Agency on the basis of public authority. That's quite understandable from everything that's been said so far. Any acts for which the adoption is solely competent, shall be adopted by the Administrative Board of the Agency on a proposal from the Director of the Agency. The President of the Governing Council shall manage the work of the Agency, represent and represent the Agency, and shall organise expert work to prepare all proposals to the Governing Council. The annual report on the overall situation in the energy sector, as well as the financial report, on the implementation of the financial plan, shall be submitted by the Agency to the Croatian Parliament and the Government of the Republic of Croatia. I stress and repeat the existing structure of the Energy Regulatory Council, according to which it consists of only five members standing here, I said three, disabling its independent and active performance in the field of energy regulation and we really had an important institution that was actually fictitious, but as the virtual institution says today, because such important responsibilities, the responsibilities of the activities of the institution with only a few employees could not be carried out. How did she do those jobs? They were carried out in the following way, that most of them, or almost all of the professional tasks for the purposes of the Council, were carried out by the Energy Institute "Croatian Fire" as an institution over which the Council had no control. In such circumstances, the Council does not have the necessary conditions or has not had the necessary conditions to exercise its functions independently of the regulatory body in the field of energy regulation and this has led to difficulties in carrying out energy activities and the establishment of energy markets with the potential for long-term serious consequences, so such a Council work organisation had to be abolished and this is what we are likely to vote on Friday and adopt. It should, in particular, be pointed out once again that the Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities in the scope of its affairs, by jurisdiction, is stronger than many ministries in the Republic of Croatia. I usually make fun of myself, it's practically one strong ministry, the Ministry of Energy, but for reasons of simpler management at the government level, that institution has a title as agreed and adopted, i.e. The Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities. Justifying such an important institution with this law is only the first step. Functioning, especially the quality functioning of this Agency is a job that will take a long time, and the first step is extremely important, i.e. the installation of quality people in view of the complexity of the tasks for which the Agency will be competent, especially the leaders. A good law doesn't have to be good in implementation. Without quality good people capable of coping with all these challenges, the Agency will not be able to meet the challenges and I hope that this issue, which is just as important as the law itself, will pay special attention. Furthermore, a few words on the proposal for a law on the electricity market. As is known, by implementing the provisions of the Electricity Market Act, and by respecting the provisions of the Energy Act, on 1 July 2002, Croatia's electricity industry was transformed into a system of related societies of basic and accompanying activities. Respecting the provisions of the Law on Commercial Companies, Croatia's electricity, as is known, is organized as a concept called HEP Group, run by the ruling company Croatia's electricity industry, and all the others below are listed companies over which Croatia's electricity has a dominant influence. It is HEP production, HEP transmission, HEP distribution and HEP supply. The adoption of the Energy Act, the Law on the Regulatory of Energy Activities and the Law on the Market in Electricity practically emanates one serious and large process of restructuring the HEP. What reasons for the restructuring of HEP according to this Law have already been said and I just want to point out once again. In particular, it is a significant unsustainable alignment with the requirements of Directive 2003-54-EC. As a result, this Law will introduce, along with a single transmission system operator, what we have said, the integration of transmission and management systems, the introduction of a distribution system operator, as well as a market operator. This is the important element since today's market operator is in HEP structure and this is in line with the European Union directives, in line with the requirements on the liberalisation of the energy market, is not acceptable. Therefore, there are very complex and important structural changes ahead of HEP for all of these reasons. Finally, such a solution as is known is a European standard. The establishment of the so-called The TSO operator is the task ahead. First of all, it would point out, especially and important because of the international obligations of the Republic of Croatia, i.e. because of the positioning of the formal and strategic Croatian transmission system operator in international frameworks. There is a near signing of important international contracts, i.e., accession to the European Association of transmission system operators, so-called ETSO. And positioning of the Croatian transmission system operator in the bodies of the European Electricity Transfer Association, UCTE. Finally, for all these reasons, the Croatian Democratic Community Club strongly supports this great effort by the Government of the Republic of Croatia to modernise the energy sector of the Republic of Croatia as a whole. And as I said, the first step in modernising this energy sector, this energy system is to adopt a certain package of laws. Again, I stress, in the end, the implementation and implementation of these laws is, I would say, far more complex tasks and more responsible than the definition of laws and their adoption. And for this reason, the Croatian Democratic Community Club expects the implementation of all these laws to be made as high-quality, organised and efficient as this first step towards modernising the legislative system of the Republic of Croatia in the energy sector. Thank you very much.

In fact, this is about a package of laws that speaks about regulation of energy activities, i.e. the electricity market and energy. This package of laws is actually the beginning of a break-up with the concept of regulating the Croatian energy market, especially the electricity created between 2000 and 2003. When this concept was adopted and I remind you during the summer extraordinary session of 2000 in the late night hours, which my colleague Slaven Letica would say, when wise owls were hunting mice, then in 2000 at that extraordinary session HSP warned of the unsustainability and harmfulness of Croatian economic interests of such concept. The whole concept was rapid and there is really nothing in the material titled the Croatian energy sector reform proposal, in which even eight pages without any financial indicator suggested such a concept of reform of the Croatian energy sector. So, reforming and liberalising the electricity system. But that is why there was no lack of much appeal then to the European Commission directives and membership in the European Union, all to actually justify the introduction of a system that had not yet been brought to life anywhere back then in 2000. And those who actually tested it and tried it like Italy and Greece now at this same time that we're discussing it, that concept now give up on it. So today we are witnessing that through these changes in the law and the Republic of Croatia such as Italy and Greece finally give up that concept from the system. The changes in the energy system are not harmless, because without any patheticity, it is literally about the economic blood flow of the state, and companies and companies in the field of energy are generally both in capital and in profit strongest in every country in the world. That is why no country is concerned about who or how or in whose interest it directs visions of the development of energy. In addition, INA and HEP are worth at least four and a half billion dollars in the most modest judgments, and any change in energy prices or oil derivatives has a dramatic impact on the economy, as well as on citizens' standards throughout the economy. However, in the Republic of Croatia everything was in the hands of the Energy Regulatory Council, which actually consists of 5 members. And for those five members of this Energy Regulatory Council, there was no sophisticated professional service, but all the professional support for their decisions was provided by the Institute of Fire. So, whether it is electricity or gas tariffs, the restructuring of INE and HEP, the planning of development, etc., all decisions were subject to the famous professional influence of the Institute "Croatia Pozar" and its director, the famous strategy of Goran Granić, and of course not for free, but equally not necessarily professionally and impartially and of course not necessarily in the national interest of Croatia. More specifically, without excess environments, the whole system has been regulated by law so far that the director of the "Croatian Fire" Institute was actually the master of Croatia's energy sector. It is therefore logical to establish a private agency with the Council as its professional service when it is already going to create an independent regulator, a liberal energy market. So why all the rush to liberalising the energy sector when no one in Croatia knows exactly how much electricity our country will need in 5 or 25 years. How much energy will we be able to recover from our investments, for example in Serbia, Bosnia and Herzegovina? No national development strategy has yet been developed. The alleged reason for the reform of the energy sector, i.e. the law on the liberalisation of the electricity market, which was discussed by the Parliament in 2000, was Croatia's involvement in European integration. But that's certainly completely pointless. Why? In particular, the 1997 European Union Directive on the liberalisation of the electricity market advocates national sovereignty over its own energy and transport systems, i.e. power lines, pipelines, pipelines, etc. In addition, the said directive provides for 2 equal underlines the equal model of regulating electricity markets, so on. The open market model, which was in fact the basis of that 2000 strategy. Based on the example of part of the EU member states and even more famous California. In other words, from the other old model of the only buyer that is in power in Croatia today. And they apply it in the European Union, including Italy, France and Ireland. So, if the Italians, French and Irish, along with this system of the only buyer, recorded economic growth within the European Union, why could it not continue to apply to Croatia? At that time, however, the government chose an open market model that envisioned the development of INA and HEP to private private companies that freely, therefore without state control, trade in energy and fuel. However, Croatia's national interest is to monitor its market energy in the model of the only buyer. Not even the most concerned supporters of the free market in the European Union or in the United States would allow themselves, for example, to be monitored by 75 to 80% of the energy market. The privatisation of HEP also meant the sale of Croatian family silver. In addition, newly created private companies will not have the conditions to survive, so they will gladly increase electricity prices differently in different parts of Croatia. The fight of private profit companies will eliminate their will to expand power plants and improve the transport network, and contrary to the current obligation of HEP to provide electricity to everyone in the territory of Croatia, this new model of the open market involves consumer freedom to choose the most favourable energy supplier, even outside Croatia. the freedom to import electricity, for example from Ukraine. But at the same time, the freedom of energy suppliers to choose a buyer of electricity outside Croatia, for example, in Italy. No matter how much Croatia really needs energy right now. So none of the privates in that model are responsible for, for example, the lack of electricity, because they actually aim at profit, i.e. exporting electricity where it can be sold without risk. All of these happened in California. After privatising and liberalising the electricity market according to the open market model and causing energy supply instability and power reductions, watch out for power cuts in California. This, however, would cause even more suffering and power reduction in Croatia than those we witnessed in 92. On the other hand, a sharp rise in electricity and fuel prices, which would completely affect the Croatian economy. Although work is fortunately far behind us, Croatia has no reason to repeat California's mistakes today. All Croatian residents with discomfort remember periods of power cuts during the war years. It is possible, however, that we will experience a reduction of electricity throughout Croatia in a few years, for example. For those who do not consider it possible, it is worth mentioning the news of the CCN as of 18 January this year in connection with the power shortage in California. So, in California with such a successful economy that it would belong to the eight richest countries in the world if, by any chance, it was independent, in January of this year even the famous Governor Schwarzenegger and his government were tortured by power shortages. California was missing 18 gigawatts a day, so just before the summer heats when the air conditioning device greatly increases energy consumption. The crisis hasn't been resolved until today. The state of California imported some electricity from other countries, and it paid off part of the debt only to the largest private distributor, a private electricity distributor in California, which is "Pacific GS and eletic", which, after all of that, was bankrupt. The governor proposed austerity measures for higher electricity consumption and increased electricity prices by 150 to 300%. Sounds very familiar to you, 'cause so? So the whole thing is out in California to the citizens, who are the least guilty of it. Of course, far from me wanting to compare Croatia with California, which is no longer necessary to doubt whether such a vital economy is going to know how to get out of this energy crisis, especially since Governor Arnold Schwarzenegger is. Yet if California could afford so much trouble from the so-called. The liberalisation and model of the open electricity market, what would happen to Croatia then? That's why we at the HSP Club repeat what we said in 2000. It must leave the open market model urgently and return to the only buyer model, such as Italy or as such a model in the European Union has France, Ireland and some other countries. And secondly, don't forget that with this package of laws here today, the proposed law on the privatisation of HEP has to be changed, otherwise nothing will be done, this will remain a dead letter on paper. Unfortunately, we lost most of our INA. Therefore, leaving the open market model and changing the HEP Privatisation Act would finally protect the Croatian electricity system from the risk of collapse and protect the Croatian economy from possible collapse. The only damage could then be suffered by Croatian Energy Strategy Goran Granic due to the decline in the value of the shares of the Institute of Hrvoje Pozar, but I think it is a damage that we can all bear. Thank you very much.

With this legislative package, Croatia is preparing a basis for a follow-up, i.e. a second step in energy reform aimed at liberalising energy markets and enabling third parties to access transmission and transport systems. The first step was taken on 19 July 2001 when the Parliament adopted the Energy Act as a roof law in the energy field and four other laws in this area. Today, however, almost five years after the adoption of these laws, it is evident that the current Energy Act is not complete, it does not cover all the problems especially of those processes and activities that have strengthened not only in Europe but also in us. Such as rationalising consumption and energy efficiency development of cogeneration, i.e. Concomitant production of electricity and heat, i.e. at least two media in the single process, the development of renewable energy sources of bio-mass and bio-fuels, energy protection issues and environmental protection with this activity. These terms are neither present in the applicable law or under-quality, so the existing legal framework has become too narrow to develop energy activities and organise energy markets, or liberalising it. In addition, coordination with a number of European Union documents adopted after 2001 should be carried out. Directive 2003/44/EC foresees a unique transmission system operator transmission system operator, tasked with transferring and managing the power system while our current Energy Act treats and activities as separate. Such a solution, ultimately, would lead to a threat to the security of the power system's drive and, therefore, this law changes the next logic of the technological unity of the existing system and introduces a single transmission system operator in Croatia. Furthermore, technical conditions have recently been created for the expansion of electricity trade in the region, which has been made public with the European Union's top representatives in the Ernestinov Trafo station, so the haste to adopt the amendment of the Energy Act is necessary in order not to halt the process of signing international contracts to prevent us from joining European associations and agreements, as well as to prevent access to funds. The electricity market law, Article 31, lays down deadlines in which customers are gradually given the free choice of electricity suppliers, which provides for this right to be extended to all customers by 1 July 2008 so that the process of opening the whole market will be completed. Of course, he was treated, but in the first stage, the normative privileged buyer from 40 gigawatt hours to 20 gigawatt hours is downgraded to customers with consumption of more than 9 gigawatt hours and on 1 July 2007 for buyers of category entrepreneurs. The energy regulation law of Articles 6 and 7 defines the establishment of the Croatian Energy Regulatory Agency HERA as an independent non-profit organisation to regulate participants and relations in the entire energy market of Croatia. This is certainly a better solution than this current ineffective regulatory council that does not have its own logistics and has not been able to fulfil the expected tasks. And without further ado, I suggest that we adopt these three energy laws by emergency procedure, as suggested, in order to initiate this process which was actually lagging behind after 2001 and did not meet expectations. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28274	Thank you, Mr. President of the Parliament. Dear ladies and gentlemen of Parliament. Here are three laws, energy laws that are very important and above all, which are changes to the energy law and new laws on electricity triangulation and the law on regulation of energy activities. Refort energy sector in the Republic of Croatia began in July 2001 when the Croatian Parliament adopted the concept of reform adopted a package of energy laws. This enables, among other things, the creation of an institutional energy sector framework with particular emphasis on energy market liberalisation. The five Directives of the European Union that came into force after 2001 are newly regulating issues in the energy sector. The additional reason for amending these laws is also the difficulties in implementing the energy sector reform under existing laws. It is necessary to redesign an existing system and market operator by setting up a transmission system operator to perform integrated power system management and power transmission functions. It is necessary to separate the market operator from HEP. Such a solution brings unique responsibility for the security of the electricity system and is in line with solutions in most European countries. Further opening of the market requires a more operational energy regulator. So short a few sentences about each of these laws. Amendments to the Energy Act. The energy law is a roof law for all laws in the energy sector and provides a comprehensive legal framework for the sector. This law sets out, first and foremost, the structure of regulated and free energy prices. Methodology for the determination of tariff items with a height of headings. And jurisdiction over its adoption which is reasonably assigned to the Government, the Ministry of Regulatory and Energy Entities to which a particular tariff system is concerned. The law also accurately formulate general conditions for energy supply and consumer protection measures. To speed up solving the problem of illegal construction, these amendments to the law prohibit the collection of buildings with a construction permit on energy networks. The electricity market law lays down regulated and marketable electricity activities. How to obtain the status of a privileged producer, and the right to encourage. As well as the procedures for granting approval for the construction of new power plants, and the publication and execution of a public tender if access is to be opened for that construction. The law lays down the responsibilities and responsibilities of transmission operator and distribution system operator. AND their independence in a vertically integrated company. The independence of electricity loads from electricity transmission and distribution activities and the obligation of all suppliers to assume electricity from renewable energy sources and generation as well as the obligation of public information providers is regulated. Special emphasis is on the protection of customers from the category of households and small customers. Additional roles of market operators in the performance of operations, e.g. the calculation of balancing energy, and the collection and distribution of funds for the promotion of renewable energy sources and cogeneration have been established. The annual spending limit has been reduced to obtaining preferential customer status, from 40 to 20 gigawatt hours, and dynamics have been established until the market is finally opened. The public service obligation of supply and the rights and obligations of privileged and tariff buyers has been established in a transparent manner. The transitional and final provisions of the law lay down the obligations of HEP for the provision of public services and for the establishment of a new structure, in particular the separation of supply and distribution. Issuing of market operators from HEP and public service obligations of customers who are left without suppliers or who do not wish to use the status of a privileged buyer. In order to establish and implement the regulation of energy activities, the system of regulated action of energy subjects, and the establishment and arrangement of the energy market by the new energy regulation law, it is proposed to establish an independent, independent, non-profit, public institutions called the Croatian Energy Regulatory Agency. The founder of the agency is proposed that the Republic of Croatia, the agency for its work, corresponds to the Croatian Assembly, this law regulates the internal structure of the agency, accurately determines and determines its business and activities as an activity of special state interest. For example, these are the tasks of disposing of permits to carry out energy activities. By providing methodologies and supervision of the application of tariff systems. Control of the work of energy subjects. All of the so far, this is important to say, the acts of the Council for the Regulatory of Energy Activities and all the solutions of that Council and the permits are valid for the time of the one issued, so it simply ensures the continuity of the current council and the new agency. The reasons why this law is important and important to adopt already and this year are not only a alignment with the European Union directives but also our international obligations and the need to be members of European associations so that we can use the funds, that we are moving towards the liberalisation of the energy sector as quickly as possible. To regulate the position of renewable energy sources and cogenerations that have not been legally regulated so far. In view of the need to establish a level of operationality in the work of regulators and normal compliance with the way energy law, electricity market law and energy regulation law. Therefore, I ask you to adopt these amendments to the law on energy and new laws on the electricity market and the law on energy regulation. Thank you.

And that full harmonisation is coming after the adoption of sublegal acts in time to Croatia's accession to the European Union. ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28278	Your Honor, Mr. President, the Honorable Minister, the Honorable Representatives. The Committee on the Economy, Development and Reconstruction of the Croatian Parliament at the 21st session held on 29 November 2004 discussed the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law on energy with the Final Proposal of the Law, the proposal for a regulation of energy activities with the Final Proposal for a Law and the Proposal for a Law on the Market of Electricity with the Final Proposal for Law, which was submitted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia by the Act of 18 November 2004, with a proposal to comply with Article 159. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament of Laws shall be adopted by an urgent procedure. The Committee shall be within the meaning of Article 67. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament discussed the above-mentioned Bills as a parent working body. On a proposal from the Committee president, the Committee discussed the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Energy Act with the Final Proposal for Law, the emergency procedure, the 1st and 2nd reading, the proposal for a law on the electricity market with the final draft law, the emergency procedure, the 1st and 2nd reading and the proposal for a law on energy regulation with the final draft law, the emergency procedure 1st and 2nd reading. In his opening statement, the applicant's representative pointed out that the regulatory energy activity process started in 2001 and that, after a short delay in these legislative proposals, the process continued. When drawing up these legislative proposals, binding guidelines of the European Union have been taken into account, in particular those 5 relating to these legislative proposals. The applicant specifically pointed out that according to the European Union directives, the energy system should be guided in a way that enables basic decisions to be made in one place. These legislative proposals prescribe guidelines for other laws to be passed and are related to energy activity. A request for a structured energy price has also been introduced, so that all consumers know what enters that price. Particularly protected are natural persons and small businesses, as energy users. It is prescribed that all users have the right to choose to buy electricity. The proposing representative also stressed the jurisdiction of the Croatian regulatory agency whose decisions are final, which means that only judicial protection can be sought against them. These legislative proposals have established a new way of calculating the transmission of electricity abroad. The adoption of these laws is a presumption of the adoption of sublegal acts in the field of energy which should result in greater liberalisation of the energy market. In the opening of the debate, the Committee President met the members of the Committee with the material of the Croatian Chamber of Commerce whose representative pointed out that HEP supports the adoption of the proposed laws, while INA and JANAF have objections which are united in that material. The basic observation by INA and JANAF is that, in view of the importance of the matter regulated by these legislative proposals, they should go into the regular procedure of adoption without the application of an emergency procedure. The representative of the Croatian Chamber of Commerce has proposed that the fundamental law relating to the harmonisation of Croatian legislation with the legislation of the European Union should also be discussed in regular procedure. The president of the trade union in Croatia's electricity industry stressed that these legal proposals begin the abolition of the HEP monopoly and continue the process of restructuring of HEP. Following the discussion, the Committee unanimously reached the next conclusion. It is supported by the adoption of the Law amending and supplementing the Energy Act, the adoption of the Energy Regulation Act and the adoption of the Electricity Market Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28280	Your Honor, Mr. President, Mr. Secretary, ladies and gentlemen. The 2001 Energy Act, as known, is a roof law for all other laws in the field of energy. It was prepared nearly 5 years ago, and it is understandable today that it is not in line with the new European Union directives adopted in 2003 and there are legitimate reasons for updating it. Because of this, it is not enough framework for all other laws in the energy sector and the efforts of the Government of Croatia to modernise and update it have been fully justified and therefore here today we are discussing this package of laws. I'll just point out some shortcomings in this law. The current Energy Act provides that energy activities are separate from the transmission of electricity and the management of the electricity system. That's not good for many reasons. Not only because of the fact that this does not correspond to European knowledge today, but simply the necessary level of synergy and uniform management of the electricity system management system and the transmission system enables far greater efficiency of the system as a whole. Furthermore, Directive 203 54 EC does not know a separate system operator from an energy transmission operator, but only a single transmission system operator, tsl TSO transmission system operator to be responsible for the following tasks: ensuring the long-term capacity of the demand system, contributing to security of supply, managing energy flows in the network, informing the operator of adjacent networks, non-discrimination of network users, informing network users etc. In addition, there are additional reasons why it was necessary to update the existing energy law. A very significant problem of rationalising energy consumption, i.e. The increase in energy efficiency is not sufficiently defined and regulated by the current Energy Act and no compliance with the European Union directives has been achieved. The relevant law further does not adequately and sufficiently clearly describe the tariff systems and the procedures for their adoption for certain energy activities. Furthermore, the applicable gas economy law does not cover the energy activity of natural gas supply. In addition, natural gas supply and natural gas distribution activities as public services do not have a regulated price tariff methodology. Furthermore, as regards oil and petroleum products, the applicable law does not include the energy activities of biofuel production, oil pipeline transport, oil and oil transport, and oil derivatives and biofuels by road vehicles. This is due to the fact that the energy operators present cannot obtain authorisations to carry out the activities for which they are otherwise qualified. Furthermore, the adoption of this law and all the laws that we are discussing today, which are related to this law, will result in the opening of the energy market according to the European Union directives and will contribute to the rapid liberalisation of the energy sector and the rationalisation of energy entities in the Republic of Croatia. I will stress only some important elements relating to harmonisation with European Union directives and documents, the adoption of common rules on the electricity market, the adoption of common rules on the internal market for natural gas, the adoption and promotion of the use of electricity from renewable energy sources, the promotion of cogeneration based on the use of useful heat and the promotion of the use of biofuels and other renewable fuels in transport. So from all that's been said, it's quite clear that for the past five years they've made important changes in the existing Energy Act. So the main objective of this bill is to better identify and regulate all energy activities in the Republic of Croatia, ensure a unique view of all energy activities, a single management and management system with all the important parts of this complex energy system in the Republic of Croatia. Establish a more comprehensive legal framework for other energy sector laws in order to make production, transfer, distribution and supply of energy to all customers in the Republic of Croatia more reliable and efficient. In addition, all documents place particular emphasis on environmental protection measures, renewable energy, cogeneration and energy efficiency. Now allow me a few words about the proposal for a law on energy regulation. Recently in this House, we discussed the Council's annual report on energy regulation. We took note of it, with certain observations which were primarily related to the fact that the Energy Regulatory Council has, in accordance with the existing law, significant means to charge for the provision and authorisation of activities in the energy sector. However, this Energy Regulatory Council had only three employees with such significant revenues and I personally suggested that we be provided with a report on the financial operations of the Energy Regulatory Council, but here we are today discussing a proposal for a law on the regulation of energy activities which, in some ways, dodging or abolishing the Energy Regulatory Council, which really makes sense and justification, I would like to say a few words. The Republic of Croatia is a signator of the European Energy Charter, which has been established to accelerate the liberalisation of the energy sector and allow third parties to access existing transmission and transport systems of energy in the Republic of Croatia. The aim of opening the market is to give all energy suppliers an equal opportunity to supply customers with objectively visible and impartial conditions, and the establishment of regulation should allow for control of the achievement of the objectives set. The proposed law, as I said about regulation of energy activities, establishes and this is highlighted in the opening section, the Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities as a public and independent institution that is structured as follows. She has her own board of directors, she needs to have her own board president, she has an agency, i.e. staff of the Agency and she should have a director as an appropriate professional. The Administrative Board of the Energy Regulatory Agency shall be appointed by the Croatian Parliament. I hope that we will have this very soon in the Croatian Parliament in the procedure, and the director is elected by the administrative council as an expert in the public competition. The Statute of the Agency shall be adopted by the Administrative Board of the Agency, with the prior consent of the Government of the Republic of Croatia. The activities of the Agency shall be of particular interest to the Republic of Croatia and shall be carried out by the Agency on the basis of public authority. That's quite understandable from everything that's been said so far. Any acts for which the adoption is solely competent, shall be adopted by the Administrative Board of the Agency on a proposal from the Director of the Agency. The President of the Governing Council shall manage the work of the Agency, represent and represent the Agency, and shall organise expert work to prepare all proposals to the Governing Council. The annual report on the overall situation in the energy sector, as well as the financial report, on the implementation of the financial plan, shall be submitted by the Agency to the Croatian Parliament and the Government of the Republic of Croatia. I stress and repeat the existing structure of the Energy Regulatory Council, according to which it consists of only five members standing here, I said three, disabling its independent and active performance in the field of energy regulation and we really had an important institution that was actually fictitious, but as the virtual institution says today, because such important responsibilities, the responsibilities of the activities of the institution with only a few employees could not be carried out. How did she do those jobs? They were carried out in the following way, that most of them, or almost all of the professional tasks for the purposes of the Council, were carried out by the Energy Institute "Croatian Fire" as an institution over which the Council had no control. In such circumstances, the Council does not have the necessary conditions or has not had the necessary conditions to exercise its functions independently of the regulatory body in the field of energy regulation and this has led to difficulties in carrying out energy activities and the establishment of energy markets with the potential for long-term serious consequences, so such a Council work organisation had to be abolished and this is what we are likely to vote on Friday and adopt. It should, in particular, be pointed out once again that the Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities in the scope of its affairs, by jurisdiction, is stronger than many ministries in the Republic of Croatia. I usually make fun of myself, it's practically one strong ministry, the Ministry of Energy, but for reasons of simpler management at the government level, that institution has a title as agreed and adopted, i.e. The Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities. Justifying such an important institution with this law is only the first step. Functioning, especially the quality functioning of this Agency is a job that will take a long time, and the first step is extremely important, i.e. the installation of quality people in view of the complexity of the tasks for which the Agency will be competent, especially the leaders. A good law doesn't have to be good in implementation. Without quality good people capable of coping with all these challenges, the Agency will not be able to meet the challenges and I hope that this issue, which is just as important as the law itself, will pay special attention. Furthermore, a few words on the proposal for a law on the electricity market. As is known, by implementing the provisions of the Electricity Market Act, and by respecting the provisions of the Energy Act, on 1 July 2002, Croatia's electricity industry was transformed into a system of related societies of basic and accompanying activities. Respecting the provisions of the Law on Commercial Companies, Croatia's electricity, as is known, is organized as a concept called HEP Group, run by the ruling company Croatia's electricity industry, and all the others below are listed companies over which Croatia's electricity has a dominant influence. It is HEP production, HEP transmission, HEP distribution and HEP supply. The adoption of the Energy Act, the Law on the Regulatory of Energy Activities and the Law on the Market in Electricity practically emanates one serious and large process of restructuring the HEP. What reasons for the restructuring of HEP according to this Law have already been said and I just want to point out once again. In particular, it is a significant unsustainable alignment with the requirements of Directive 2003-54-EC. As a result, this Law will introduce, along with a single transmission system operator, what we have said, the integration of transmission and management systems, the introduction of a distribution system operator, as well as a market operator. This is the important element since today's market operator is in HEP structure and this is in line with the European Union directives, in line with the requirements on the liberalisation of the energy market, is not acceptable. Therefore, there are very complex and important structural changes ahead of HEP for all of these reasons. Finally, such a solution as is known is a European standard. The establishment of the so-called The TSO operator is the task ahead. First of all, it would point out, especially and important because of the international obligations of the Republic of Croatia, i.e. because of the positioning of the formal and strategic Croatian transmission system operator in international frameworks. There is a near signing of important international contracts, i.e., accession to the European Association of transmission system operators, so-called ETSO. And positioning of the Croatian transmission system operator in the bodies of the European Electricity Transfer Association, UCTE. Finally, for all these reasons, the Croatian Democratic Community Club strongly supports this great effort by the Government of the Republic of Croatia to modernise the energy sector of the Republic of Croatia as a whole. And as I said, the first step in modernising this energy sector, this energy system is to adopt a certain package of laws. Again, I stress, in the end, the implementation and implementation of these laws is, I would say, far more complex tasks and more responsible than the definition of laws and their adoption. And for this reason, the Croatian Democratic Community Club expects the implementation of all these laws to be made as high-quality, organised and efficient as this first step towards modernising the legislative system of the Republic of Croatia in the energy sector. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28284	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Gentlemen and Madam Representatives. It's really a lot of times I'd say fun or less fun to listen to the same subject change attitudes and thinking depending on whether you're in power or you're in opposition. In fact, this is about a package of laws that speaks about regulation of energy activities, i.e. the electricity market and energy. This package of laws is actually the beginning of a break-up with the concept of regulating the Croatian energy market, especially the electricity created between 2000 and 2003. When this concept was adopted and I remind you during the summer extraordinary session of 2000 in the late night hours, which my colleague Slaven Letica would say, when wise owls were hunting mice, then in 2000 at that extraordinary session HSP warned of the unsustainability and harmfulness of Croatian economic interests of such concept. The whole concept was rapid and there is really nothing in the material titled the Croatian energy sector reform proposal, in which even eight pages without any financial indicator suggested such a concept of reform of the Croatian energy sector. So, reforming and liberalising the electricity system. But that is why there was no lack of much appeal then to the European Commission directives and membership in the European Union, all to actually justify the introduction of a system that had not yet been brought to life anywhere back then in 2000. And those who actually tested it and tried it like Italy and Greece now at this same time that we're discussing it, that concept now give up on it. So today we are witnessing that through these changes in the law and the Republic of Croatia such as Italy and Greece finally give up that concept from the system. The changes in the energy system are not harmless, because without any patheticity, it is literally about the economic blood flow of the state, and companies and companies in the field of energy are generally both in capital and in profit strongest in every country in the world. That is why no country is concerned about who or how or in whose interest it directs visions of the development of energy. In addition, INA and HEP are worth at least four and a half billion dollars in the most modest judgments, and any change in energy prices or oil derivatives has a dramatic impact on the economy, as well as on citizens' standards throughout the economy. However, in the Republic of Croatia everything was in the hands of the Energy Regulatory Council, which actually consists of 5 members. And for those five members of this Energy Regulatory Council, there was no sophisticated professional service, but all the professional support for their decisions was provided by the Institute of Fire. So, whether it is electricity or gas tariffs, the restructuring of INE and HEP, the planning of development, etc., all decisions were subject to the famous professional influence of the Institute "Croatia Pozar" and its director, the famous strategy of Goran Granić, and of course not for free, but equally not necessarily professionally and impartially and of course not necessarily in the national interest of Croatia. More specifically, without excess environments, the whole system has been regulated by law so far that the director of the "Croatian Fire" Institute was actually the master of Croatia's energy sector. It is therefore logical to establish a private agency with the Council as its professional service when it is already going to create an independent regulator, a liberal energy market. So why all the rush to liberalising the energy sector when no one in Croatia knows exactly how much electricity our country will need in 5 or 25 years. How much energy will we be able to recover from our investments, for example in Serbia, Bosnia and Herzegovina? No national development strategy has yet been developed. The alleged reason for the reform of the energy sector, i.e. the law on the liberalisation of the electricity market, which was discussed by the Parliament in 2000, was Croatia's involvement in European integration. But that's certainly completely pointless. Why? In particular, the 1997 European Union Directive on the liberalisation of the electricity market advocates national sovereignty over its own energy and transport systems, i.e. power lines, pipelines, pipelines, etc. In addition, the said directive provides for 2 equal underlines the equal model of regulating electricity markets, so on. The open market model, which was in fact the basis of that 2000 strategy. Based on the example of part of the EU member states and even more famous California. In other words, from the other old model of the only buyer that is in power in Croatia today. And they apply it in the European Union, including Italy, France and Ireland. So, if the Italians, French and Irish, along with this system of the only buyer, recorded economic growth within the European Union, why could it not continue to apply to Croatia? At that time, however, the government chose an open market model that envisioned the development of INA and HEP to private private companies that freely, therefore without state control, trade in energy and fuel. However, Croatia's national interest is to monitor its market energy in the model of the only buyer. Not even the most concerned supporters of the free market in the European Union or in the United States would allow themselves, for example, to be monitored by 75 to 80% of the energy market. The privatisation of HEP also meant the sale of Croatian family silver. In addition, newly created private companies will not have the conditions to survive, so they will gladly increase electricity prices differently in different parts of Croatia. The fight of private profit companies will eliminate their will to expand power plants and improve the transport network, and contrary to the current obligation of HEP to provide electricity to everyone in the territory of Croatia, this new model of the open market involves consumer freedom to choose the most favourable energy supplier, even outside Croatia. the freedom to import electricity, for example from Ukraine. But at the same time, the freedom of energy suppliers to choose a buyer of electricity outside Croatia, for example, in Italy. No matter how much Croatia really needs energy right now. So none of the privates in that model are responsible for, for example, the lack of electricity, because they actually aim at profit, i.e. exporting electricity where it can be sold without risk. All of these happened in California. After privatising and liberalising the electricity market according to the open market model and causing energy supply instability and power reductions, watch out for power cuts in California. This, however, would cause even more suffering and power reduction in Croatia than those we witnessed in 92. On the other hand, a sharp rise in electricity and fuel prices, which would completely affect the Croatian economy. Although work is fortunately far behind us, Croatia has no reason to repeat California's mistakes today. All Croatian residents with discomfort remember periods of power cuts during the war years. It is possible, however, that we will experience a reduction of electricity throughout Croatia in a few years, for example. For those who do not consider it possible, it is worth mentioning the news of the CCN as of 18 January this year in connection with the power shortage in California. So, in California with such a successful economy that it would belong to the eight richest countries in the world if, by any chance, it was independent, in January of this year even the famous Governor Schwarzenegger and his government were tortured by power shortages. California was missing 18 gigawatts a day, so just before the summer heats when the air conditioning device greatly increases energy consumption. The crisis hasn't been resolved until today. The state of California imported some electricity from other countries, and it paid off part of the debt only to the largest private distributor, a private electricity distributor in California, which is "Pacific GS and eletic", which, after all of that, was bankrupt. The governor proposed austerity measures for higher electricity consumption and increased electricity prices by 150 to 300%. Sounds very familiar to you, 'cause so? So the whole thing is out in California to the citizens, who are the least guilty of it. Of course, far from me wanting to compare Croatia with California, which is no longer necessary to doubt whether such a vital economy is going to know how to get out of this energy crisis, especially since Governor Arnold Schwarzenegger is. Yet if California could afford so much trouble from the so-called. The liberalisation and model of the open electricity market, what would happen to Croatia then? That's why we at the HSP Club repeat what we said in 2000. It must leave the open market model urgently and return to the only buyer model, such as Italy or as such a model in the European Union has France, Ireland and some other countries. And secondly, don't forget that with this package of laws here today, the proposed law on the privatisation of HEP has to be changed, otherwise nothing will be done, this will remain a dead letter on paper. Unfortunately, we lost most of our INA. The reform of the energy sector and, in particular, the reform of the electricity sector represents an extremely complex task, which is very likely the most complex task facing the energy sector in Croatia throughout its history. It is necessary to introduce entirely new relations, define new entities and establish a completely new legal and other regulation in this sector. The Croatian energy sector has just over a year of experience in introducing market relations on the energy sector. There is a need to build institutional relations between entities in the energy sector, which is a particularly delicate task for each country, given the existing universal solutions and with regard to the models in which each country adapts to tradition, heritage and organisation. It should be borne in mind that the energy sector is just a necessary assumption for a smooth development of the economy and society as a whole and that as such is not the purpose of itself. The Energy Regulatory Act aims to open the market, introduce competition, give continuous conditions to all suppliers, and establish regulation that allows for monitoring of the objectives set. It is certainly the most difficult task to protect customers and energy operators, and this goal is achieved only with the assumption of a successful competition, therefore competition. This means that the way to entrepreneurship in the field of energy is not easy. He's ruthless, but he's inevitable. So the definition of non-profit by the term "proposer" would actually be state, and when we talk about the budget deficit, that's when it's measured in billions of dollars or euros. However, it must be pointed out that the functional and legal independence of energy entities under the same Directive is not linked to ownership, i.e. does not necessarily assume the transfer of ownership to a specific entity. Thus, the independence of the energy system is also highly questionable in this way. Furthermore, I would like to say, regarding the organisation of the work of the Council, therefore the existing Energy Regulatory Council, has so far carried out a number of activities of considerable importance and scope, but it is necessary to consider the necessary personnel teaming and organisation and restructuring of this Council. In particular, it should be borne in mind that the current organisation, the organisational implementation of Council expert support, is unfavourable in terms of the European Commission. In order to bring this regulatory regulation of energy activities in line with European Union directives, this law establishes the Agency for the Regulatory of Energy Activities as a public and independent institution, which has an administrative council headed by the Director. The Agency's core tasks will be to monitor the overall energy system, issue energy performance permits, approve plans for development and construction of the network, control of tariff systems and all other energy entities. When we are actually discussing it this way, it seems that the process of setting up and acting of the Agency is simple and that actually the Agency can start soon or immediately after the New Year. ParlaMint-HR_2004-11-30-0.u28304	Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, ladies and gentlemen of Parliament, Mr. Minister. We have three energy laws before us, the Energy Act, that is, its amendments and additions to the Electricity Market Act and the Energy Regulatory Act. With this legislative package, Croatia is preparing a basis for a follow-up, i.e. a second step in energy reform aimed at liberalising energy markets and enabling third parties to access transmission and transport systems. The first step was taken on 19 July 2001 when the Parliament adopted the Energy Act as a roof law in the energy field and four other laws in this area. Today, however, almost five years after the adoption of these laws, it is evident that the current Energy Act is not complete, it does not cover all the problems especially of those processes and activities that have strengthened not only in Europe but also in us. Such as rationalising consumption and energy efficiency development of cogeneration, i.e. Concomitant production of electricity and heat, i.e. at least two media in the single process, the development of renewable energy sources of bio-mass and bio-fuels, energy protection issues and environmental protection with this activity. These terms are neither present in the applicable law or under-quality, so the existing legal framework has become too narrow to develop energy activities and organise energy markets, or liberalising it. In addition, coordination with a number of European Union documents adopted after 2001 should be carried out. Directive 2003/44/EC foresees a unique transmission system operator transmission system operator, tasked with transferring and managing the power system while our current Energy Act treats and activities as separate. Such a solution, ultimately, would lead to a threat to the security of the power system's drive and, therefore, this law changes the next logic of the technological unity of the existing system and introduces a single transmission system operator in Croatia. Furthermore, technical conditions have recently been created for the expansion of electricity trade in the region, which has been made public with the European Union's top representatives in the Ernestinov Trafo station, so the haste to adopt the amendment of the Energy Act is necessary in order not to halt the process of signing international contracts to prevent us from joining European associations and agreements, as well as to prevent access to funds. The electricity market law, Article 31, lays down deadlines in which customers are gradually given the free choice of electricity suppliers, which provides for this right to be extended to all customers by 1 July 2008 so that the process of opening the whole market will be completed. Of course, he was treated, but in the first stage, the normative privileged buyer from 40 gigawatt hours to 20 gigawatt hours is downgraded to customers with consumption of more than 9 gigawatt hours and on 1 July 2007 for buyers of category entrepreneurs. The energy regulation law of Articles 6 and 7 defines the establishment of the Croatian Energy Regulatory Agency HERA as an independent non-profit organisation to regulate participants and relations in the entire energy market of Croatia. This is certainly a better solution than this current ineffective regulatory council that does not have its own logistics and has not been able to fulfil the expected tasks. And without further ado, I suggest that we adopt these three energy laws by emergency procedure, as suggested, in order to initiate this process which was actually lagging behind after 2001 and did not meet expectations. Thank you.
